Let's derive a principed iterative algorithm:
Normalize p (sum=1), minimize T=max_k c_k, c=P^2.

Let's think of optimization methods details.
Define p (sum 1, nonnegative). Convolution q = p*p (length 2n-1). Objective T = max q. We can use "multiplicative weight" update to reduce high q_k:
For each high lag k, q_k =∑ p_i p_{k-i}. To reduce q_k, we can apply updates p_i ← p_i * exp(-η p_{k-i}) for all i? This is like minimizing ∑ q_k^2. If q_k large, gradient with respect to log p_i =∂(log? maybe q_k)/∂ log p_i = p_i ∂q_k/∂p_i ≈ p_i p_{k-i}+... = contribution? Actually q_k = p_i p_j + ...; derivative wrt log p_i = p_i ∂q_k/∂p_i = p_i (p_{k-i}+δ) = contribution from pairs with i. So gradient of q_k in log p is the contribution vector. To reduce max, we can subtract η times weighted contributions to log p for active lags, then renormalize. This is multiplicative gradient descent on smooth sum of exponentials. For uniform weights over all lags, update p_i ← p_i exp(-η ∑_k κ_k p_{k-i}) where κ_k ~ softmax(q_k/τ). For uniform q, gradient maybe uniform? But high q near center gets more κ, shift p.

But computing the vector h_i = ∑_k κ_k p_{k-i} is a cross-correlation/convolution between p and κ (or reversed). Can do FFT O(n log n). Since q=p*p. Let κ length 2n-1 nonnegative normalized. h_i =∑_{k} κ_k p_{k-i} (valid). This is cross-correlation, equivalent convolve p with κ_rev. Use FFT.
Then update log p_i -= η h_i (or multiply by exp(-η h_i)). Then enforce p_i≥0, renormalize. This is like inf-projection onto max? If κ_k = max indicator, this is subgradient. If κ_k = softmax(q/τ) with τ small, smooth.

Will this optimize? Need choose schedule. Start from 1/√? q has max ~? For p_i=1/√i normalized, q profile? For k≤n maybe constant ~0.785/n? Let's compute continuous q constant on [0,1] and decreasing [1,2]. Max on [0,1] and maybe t=1. κ flat over [0,1]. Update may modify p. It might find better.

Alternative minimize L∞ via "Z-OutS"? We can implement alternating directions:
- Compute q=p*p using FFT (full).
- Compute active weights w_k = softplus or top q.
- Gradient in linear p: g_i =∑_k w_k p_{k-i} (+diag).
- Update p_i = p_i * exp(-η g_i / scale) (or p_i - η g_i projected). Renormalize.

Computing objective efficiently:
```
def conv_full_fft(a):
    n=len(a)
    m=1<<(2*n-1-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,m)
    fa*=fa
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa,m)[:2*n-1]
    c[np.abs(c)<1e-12]=0
    return c
```
For many iterations, matrix m fixed. Could precompute fft plan? np.fft no plan. We can use scipy.fft with workers? 1 CPU. rfft fine.

- To avoid overflow in update, handle log p with floor. Maintain logp array (finite). Initialize p>0 with floor e.g. 1e-15. logp=log(p).
- After update, p=exp(logp - maxlogp) to avoid overflow; normalize sum1.
- But some coordinates may become extremely small; that's okay. Could set floor after log update: logp = np.maximum(logp, log_floor) ~ -30? But if too high may prevent true zeros; zeros allowed but might not be optimal? We can allow floor `1e-15` (log -34). Good.

Let's design a `polish(p0, time_limit, seed)` that does:
```
logp = log(p0+floor)
p = exp normalized
best = p; best_score = score(p)
beta sequence annealing: from 50 to 5000 (or top fraction)
for step in range(max_iters until time):
   q = conv_fft(p)
   score = 2*n*q.max()  # sum=1
   update best
   # weights
   if mode uniform: w = ones
   else:
      # use top fraction: K changes 0.1..0.7 L
      K = int(frac*L)
      w = np.zeros(L); w[topk]=1; w/=K
      # optionally blend with exponential
   grad = convolution p with w_rev + diag
   centered = grad - grad.mean()
   scale = np.max(np.abs(centered)) + eps
   # choose step = base * (1 + noise)
   logp -= step * centered / scale
   logp = np.clip(logp, log_floor, log_max)
   p = exp(logp - logp.max()); p/=sum
   # occasional random perturb in logp
```
But choosing scale=max may make updates tiny if centered has few huge; perhaps use `scale = np.mean(centered**2)**0.5` or `np.percentile`. Step likely 0.2-2. Need tune. Since grad scale? (since centered/scale O1). That changes p multiplicatively e^-O1 maybe too big. But normalizing subtract mean: relative changes factor e^{±step}. Step 0.1 safe. Use step ~0.1-0.5.

Detailed helper functions:
```
def next_pow2(x): return 1 << (int(x)-1).bit_length()

def fft_convolve(a, nfft=None):
    n=len(a)
    if nfft is None: nfft=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,nfft)
    fa*=fa
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa,nfft, n=2*n-1) # irfft can parameter n? yes
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    return c
```
`np.fft.irfft(fa,n)` returns length n. Good.
For grad cross:
```
def gradient_weights(w, p, nfft=None):
    n=len(p); L=2*n-1
    wr = w[::-1]
    if nfft is None: nfft=next_pow2(L+n-1)
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p,nfft)
    fw=np.fft.rfft(wr,nfft)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fp*fw,nfft)
    # c length L+n-1 =3n-2
    # h_i = c[L-1-i]

```

Weights w:
```
def make_weights(q, mode, beta, top_frac):
    L=len(q)
    if mode == 'top':
       K=max(1,int(top_frac*L))
       w=np.zeros(L)
       # topk indices
       # np.argpartition for speed
       idx=np.argpartition(q, -K)[-K:]
       w[idx]=1.0
       w/=w.sum()
    elif mode == 'power':
       # use log q
       qpos=np.maximum(q,1e-300)
       logw=r*np.log(qpos)
       logw-=logw.max()
       w=np.exp(logw); w/=w.sum()
    else: # softmax
       x=beta*(q-q.max())
       w=np.exp(x); w/=w.sum()
    return w
```
For top mode, using only top K can cause discontinuities. Maybe use combined: w_top + w_power. We can blend.

```
def make_power(n,a):
   idx=np.arange(1,n+1)
   p=idx**(-a)
   # maybe shift to avoid 0
   return normalize(p)
```
Taper:
```
def make_taper(n,a,r):
   idx=np.arange(1,n+1)
   x=np.arange(n)/n
   p=idx**(-a) * np.exp(-r*x/r?) 
```
Use positive:
- p = idx**-0.5 * (1 - r*x) if r<1 else idx**-0.5 * np.exp(-r*x)
- p = idx**-0.5 * (1 + r*x)
- p = idx**-0.5 + r
- mix: 0.5 idx^-0.5 + 0.5 reverse

`polish` details:
Inputs p0, time_limit, rng, global best array/score.
We need pass best by reference? Python use list or return. Helper top-level can return best, best_score.
```
def polish(p0, deadline, rng, best_p, best_score):
    p = normalize(p0.copy())
    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    q = fft_convolve(p)
    score = local_score(p)
    if score < best_score: update
    # parameters
    L=2*n-1
    beta = 50.0
    top_frac=0.5
    step=0.3
    iter=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        q = fft_convolve(p)
        score = 2*n*q.max() # sum1
        if score < best_score: best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score; print(...)
        # update weights
        # Use combination of top K and power.
        K = int(np.clip(L*(0.05 + 0.35*np.exp(-iter/5000)), 10, L)) # anneal from 40% to 5%
        # Maybe use top K plus 10% power
        w_top=...
        w_pow=...
        w=0.7*w_top+0.3*w_pow
        # or softmax with beta=step? 
        grad = gradient_weights(w,p)
        centered=grad-grad.mean()
        scale = np.sqrt(np.mean(centered*centered)) +1e-12
        # adaptive step: try small if recent bad
        logp_new = logp - step*centered/scale
        # occasional noise
        if iter%100==0:
            sigma=0.05*(1+ ...); logp_new += rng.normal(0,sigma,n)
        logp_new = np.clip(logp_new, -35, 30)
        p_new = exp(logp_new - max); p_new/=sum
        # evaluate score
        q_new=fft_convolve(p_new); score_new=...
        if score_new < score or rng.random()<accept_prob: accept; step*=1.03
        else: reject; step*=0.8
        if step < 0.02: step=0.05; # random restart small
        iter+=1
    return best_p,best_score
```

```
def smooth_interp(coarse, n):
    m=len(coarse)
    # x_coarse = (np.arange(m)+0.5)/m
    # x_fine = (np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
    # use PCHIP? scipy.interpolate.pchip_interpolate to preserve positivity? coefficients nonnegative; ppositive.
    from scipy.interpolate import PchipInterpolator
    if m==1: return full
    xp=(np.arange(m)+0.5)/m
    fp=coarse
    # ensure endpoints positive, set fp maybe max(fp, small)
    # PCHIP can produce negative if fp? fp positive; monotone? It preserves shape but may be negative at endpoints extrapolate? Use endpoints: xp append 0 -> fp[0], 1 -> fp[-1]
    x0=np.concatenate(([0.0], xp, [1.0]))
    f0=np.concatenate(([fp[0]], fp, [fp[-1]]))
    coeff=PchipInterpolator(x0,f0)
    xf=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
    p=coeff(xf)
    p=np.maximum(p,0)
    return normalize(p)
```
But PCHIP may not have endpoints exact? fine.
Alternatively simple linear interpolation with padding endpoints:
```
xfine=np.linspace(0,1,n); p=np.interp(fxfine, xp, fp, left=fp[0], right=fp[-1])
```
Linear simpler, allowed numpy. Coarse p may have sharp shape; linear okay.

Let's implement `make_top_weights(q, frac)` using `argpartition`. For K=1, return normalized one-hot. For K=L, uniform.
```
def top_weights(q, frac):
    L=len(q)
    K=max(1,int(np.floor(frac*L)))
    w=np.zeros(L)
    if K >= L:
        w.fill(1.0); return w/L
    idx=np.argpartition(q, -K)[-K:]
    w[idx]=1.0
    w /= K
    return w
```
Power weights:
```
def power_weights(q, r):
    qpos=np.maximum(q,0)
    # if all zero? not.
    # use log to avoid underflow
    logq=np.log(qpos + 1e-300)
    logw=r*logq
    logw -= logw.max()
    w=np.exp(logw)
    s=w.sum()
    if s<=0: w.fill(1); s=L
    return w/s
```
Blend:
```
def make_weights(q, frac, power_r, top_weight_frac):
   w1=top_weights(q, frac)
   w2=power_weights(q, power_r)
   w=top_weight_frac*w1 + (1-top_weight_frac)*w2
   return w/w.sum()
```
Maybe top_weight_frac =0.8.
Anneal frac from 0.4 to0.05, power_r from2 to8.

```
w = w*(1-0.05) + np.full(L, 0.05/L) # tiny uniform to include all
```
Actually uniform normalized: `w = mix*w + (1-mix)*(1/L)*ones`. This keeps low lags.

Implementation of update:
```
def mirror_step(p, grad, step, rng, sigma=0.0, floor=1e-300):
    centered = grad - np.mean(grad)
    scale = np.mean(centered**2)**0.5 + 1e-16
    direction = centered / scale
    logp = np.log(p + floor)
    logp = logp - step * direction
    if sigma > 0:
        logp = logp + rng.normal(0, sigma, len(logp))
    # optional clip absolute
    logp = np.clip(logp, -35, 20)
    maxl = logp.max()
    pnew = np.exp(logp - maxl)
    pnew = np.maximum(pnew, 1e-15)
    pnew /= pnew.sum()
    return pnew
```
But clipping absolute to -35 may reset small p to floor every step, causing bias. Since logp after normalization have max ~0 (p max <1), values can be -35 if tiny. Clipping -35 okay. If a coordinate should be smaller than 1e-15, not important. Use floor 1e-12? Maybe too high. Use -30.
If p has max ~0.1 log -2; min log -30; range 28 exp 1e12. okay.
Maybe not clip upper? logp mean negative; subtract step direction can make some positive; subtract max later. Clip absolute to [-40, 40] safe. Use `log_floor=-40`.

What step scale? `direction` RMS 1. Step 0.2 gives log changes up to maybe 1 (if direction peaked), factor 2.7. Okay. Use step=0.1 safer. Momentum maybe step 0.2.
Let's set base step=0.15, max=1.0, min=0.02.

Pseudo polish:
```
def polish(p0, deadline, rng, best_state, tag):
    p = normalize(p0.copy())
    q = conv_fft(p); cur=local_score(p)
    update best
    it=0; temp=1e-3; step=0.15; vel=zeros; stuck=0
    start_time=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       if it % 50 ==0:
          q=conv(p); cur=score
          if cur<best: update
          # weights based on q
       else reuse q? After accept, q_new already computed. We can keep q.
       frac=0.4*exp(-it/3000)+0.05
       w = top_weights(q, frac); w = 0.9*w +0.1*uniform
       grad = gradient_weights(w,p)
       direction = gradient_descent_direction(grad)
       if it % 100 == 0: sigma=0.03
       else 0
       p_new = mirror_step(p, direction, step, vel, sigma)
       q_new=conv(p_new); score_new=score(p_new)
       if score_new < cur + temp:
           p=p_new; q=q_new; cur=score_new; temp*=0.999; step=min(1.0,step*1.01); vel=...
           if cur<best: update; stuck=0
           else stuck+=1
       else:
           temp*=0.99; step=max(0.02,step*0.95); vel*=0.5; stuck+=1
       if stuck > 200:
           # restart from best with noise
           p = add_noise(best_state[0], rng, sigma=0.1)
           q=conv(p); cur=score(p); step=0.1; temp=1e-3; stuck=0; it=0? 
       if maybe time for official verification...
       it+=1
```
But computing q at start and after each candidate. If reject, q remains current. Good.
Need update weights from current q. If current q not computed after restart, do.

However, if we reject a candidate, we still did a candidate conv. Iteration cost two conv (current q already). Using q_new after accept. Good.

Let's define `add_noise_to_p(p, rng, sigma)` in log:
```
def add_noise_p(p, rng, sigma):
    logp=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
    logp += rng.normal(0,sigma,len(logp))
    logp = np.clip(logp, -40, 20)
    maxl=logp.max()
    pnew=np.exp(logp-maxl)
    pnew=np.maximum(pnew,1e-15); pnew/=sum
    return pnew
```
Block perturb:
```
def block_perturb(p, rng, sigma):
    cand=p.copy()
    # choose 1-3 blocks
    for _ in range(1+rng.integers(0,2)):
       width=int(rng.integers(1, max(2,len(p)//10)))
       start=int(rng.integers(0, len(p)-width))
       factor=np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma))
       cand[start:start+width] *= factor
    # maybe smooth: cand = 0.9*cand +0.1*np.convolve? not
    return normalize(cand)
```
But if using local search, gradient polish maybe enough; block can escape.

```
def make_initial_candidates(n):
    cands=[]
    idx=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=float)
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    x01=np.arange(n,dtype=float)/max(1,n-1)
    for a in [0.4,0.45,0.5,0.55,0.6]:
        cands.append(normalize(idx**(-a)))
    for r in [0.0,0.25,0.5,1.0,1.5]:
        p=idx**(-0.5)*np.exp(-r*x)
        cands.append(normalize(p))
    for r in [0.0,0.25,0.5,1.0]:
        p=idx**(-0.5)*(1.0+r*x)
        cands.append(normalize(p))
    for r in [0.1,0.3,0.5,0.8]:
        p=idx**(-0.5)+r
        cands.append(normalize(p))
    mix=0.5
    left=idx**(-0.5); right=left[::-1]
    cands.append(normalize(mix*left+(1-mix)*right)) # palindromic U? maybe high but test
    # random initial from rng? Use fixed rng from seed? Could include deterministic random.
    return cands
```

`polish` implementation details with no lambda:
```
def polish(p0, deadline, rng, best_state, tag=''):
    p = normalize(np.asarray(p0, dtype=float).copy())
    n = len(p)
    q = convolution(p)
    cur = local_score_from_q(p, q)
    update_best(best_state, p, cur, tag)
    step = 0.15
    temp = 5e-4
    stuck = 0
    it = 0
    mode_frac = 0.4
    last_old = time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       if it == 0 or it % 25 == 0:
          # recompute q just in case? We have current q from accepted candidate; but after no update maybe same. Use existing.
          if cur < best_state[1]: update (already done at accept)
       # adapt frac
       frac = 0.05 + 0.40*np.exp(-it/4000.0)
       w = make_weights(q, frac, 0.9)
       grad = gradient_for_weights(p, w)
       sigma = 0.0
       if it % 80 == 0:
          sigma = 0.05
       p_new = mirror_step(p, grad, step, rng, sigma)
       q_new = convolution(p_new)
       score_new = local_score_from_q(p_new, q_new)
       accepted = False
       if np.isfinite(score_new) and score_new <= cur + temp:
          p = p_new; q = q_new; cur = score_new
          step = min(1.0, step*1.02)
          temp = max(1e-6, temp*0.999)
          stuck = 0
          accepted = True
          if cur < best_state[1]: update_best(...)
       else:
          step = max(0.02, step*0.95)
          temp = max(1e-7, temp*0.99)
          stuck += 1
       if stuck > 250:
          # restart from best with noise
          p = add_noise(best_state[0], rng, 0.15)
          q = convolution(p); cur = local_score_from_q(p,q)
          step = 0.12; temp=1e-3; stuck=0; it=0
          # (do not update best unless better)
       if it % 2000 == 0:
          # maybe official verify current p? But expensive; do once per 2s
          pass
       it += 1
    return best_state
```
Need `local_score_from_q(a,q)` to avoid recomputing sum: `s=a.sum(); return 2*len(a)*q.max()/(s*s)` if s>0. Since p sum1, can use 2*n*q.max(). But after normalization sum=1. Use general.
`update_best` should possibly use official? We'll use local.

```
def update_best(best_state, p, score, tag=''):

        best_state[0]=p.copy(); best_state[1]=float(score)

```

`make_weights` top + uniform:
```
def make_weights(q, frac, top_mix):
    L=len(q)
    w_top=top_weights(q, frac)
    w = top_mix * w_top + (1-top_mix) / L * np.ones(L) # (1-top_mix)/L sums 0? Wait uniform weights should sum to 1. If add (1-top_mix)*(1/L)*ones, sum of uniform part =1-top_mix. w_top sum1. Total sum top_mix +1-top_mix=1. So `w = top_mix*w_top + (1-top_mix)*(1/L)*np.ones(L)`. Good.
    s=w.sum(); if s>0 w/=s; else uniform
    return w
```

`gradient_for_weights` direct for n<256:
```
def gradient_for_weights(p,w):
    n=len(p); L=2*n-1
    if n < 256:
       h=np.empty(n)
       for i in range(n):
           h[i]=np.dot(p, w[i:i+n])
    else:
       nfft=next_pow2(3*n-2)
       fp=np.fft.rfft(p,nfft)
       wr=w[::-1]
       fw=np.fft.rfft(wr,nfft)
       c=np.fft.irfft(fp*fw,nfft)
       h=c[n-1:L][::-1]
    w_even = w[0:L:2]
    return h + w_even*p
```
For n=256, direct O65536 dot each? loop 256*256 =65k, okay. For 512 maybe 262k, okay but use FFT threshold 512? Use <128 maybe to avoid Python loop overhead. For coarse m=64 use direct; 64*64=4096 negligible. For m=256 direct 65k, maybe 0.1ms in Python loop? Dot in C per i, 256 calls, okay. Use threshold 256.

Check FFT slice for n small if using FFT: L=2n-1, stop L. `c[n-1:L]` length L-(n-1)=n. Good.

`convolution(a)`: If a length n, if n<512 use direct `np.convolve(a,a)`. But np.convolve for lists? a np array; returns float. Direct O(n^2). For n=512 ~262k, fine. Use threshold 384? For coarse DE many evals, direct faster than FFT. For n=3809, FFT.
```
def convolution(a):
   n=len(a)
   if n < 512:
      c=np.convolve(a,a)
   else:
      m=2*n-1
      nfft=next_pow2(m)
      fa=np.fft.rfft(a,nfft)
      fa*=fa
      c=np.fft.irfft(fa,nfft)[:m]
   # Clean tiny imag? irfft real.
   c[c<0 & c>-1e-15]=0? Use np.maximum(c,0)
   return np.maximum(c,0)
```
If p has small negative? p nonnegative. FFT may produce negative larger? Use maximum.

`local_score_from_q(a,q)` general:
```
s=np.sum(a)
if s <= 0: return np.inf
return 2.0*len(a)*float(np.max(q))/(s*s)
```
If s<0.01 (official inf), our p sum1. For official verification, if sum<0.01 return inf.

`normalize`:
```
def normalize(p):
    p=np.asarray(p,dtype=float).copy()
    p=np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p=np.maximum(p,0.0)
    s=np.sum(p)
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(len(p),dtype=float)/len(p)
    p/=s
    return p
```

`add_noise`:
```
def add_noise(p, rng, sigma):
    p=np.asarray(p,dtype=float)
    logp=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
    logp = logp + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, len(logp))
    logp = np.clip(logp, -40.0, 20.0)
    maxl=logp.max()
    pnew=np.exp(logp-maxl)
    pnew=np.maximum(pnew,1e-15)
    pnew/=pnew.sum()
    return pnew
```
If sigma=0, still floor.

`mirror_step` similar but uses grad:
```
def mirror_step(p, grad, step, rng, sigma):
    centered=grad - np.mean(grad)
    rms=np.sqrt(np.mean(centered*centered))
    if rms <= 1e-18:
       direction=np.zeros_like(centered)
    else:
       direction=centered/rms
    logp=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
    logp = logp - step*direction
    if sigma>0:
       logp = logp + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, len(logp))
    logp = np.clip(logp, -40.0, 20.0)
    maxl=logp.max()
    pnew=np.exp(logp-maxl)
    pnew=np.maximum(pnew,1e-15)
    s=pnew.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300: return normalize(p)
    pnew/=s
    return pnew
```
Is centering gradient by mean enough to preserve sum? In log space, sum preserved first order; normalization exact. Good.

Potential problem: direction may have mean not exactly zero after clipping? centered mean zero. If grad has NaN? no.

`polish` weight frac: Since q of p sum1, max ~1e-4. Top K selection robust. Use `top_mix=0.95` maybe.

`block_search`:
```
def block_search(p0, deadline, rng, best_state):
    p=add_noise(p0,rng,0.02)
    q=convolution(p); cur=local_score_from_q(p,q)
    update_best(...)
    sigma=0.2
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       c=block_perturb(p,rng,sigma)
       q=convolution(c); sc=local_score_from_q(c,q)
       if sc < cur:
           p=c; cur=sc; update_best; sigma=min(0.5,sigma*1.05)
       else:
           # if close accept? maybe
           if sc < cur + 1e-6:
              p=c; cur=sc
           else:
              sigma=max(0.05,sigma*0.98)
    return best_state
```

Coarse DE:
Top-level objective:
```
def de_objective(x, m):
    p=np.asarray(x,dtype=float)
    p=np.maximum(p,0.0)
    s=np.sum(p)
    if s <= 1e-12: return 1e9
    p/=s
    q=convolution(p)
    return 2.0*m*float(np.max(q))
```
Need no lambda. DE may pass x array, args m.
```
def de_coarse_search(m, max_seconds, rng):
    try:
       from scipy.optimize import differential_evolution
    except Exception:
       return None
    start=time.monotonic()
    deadline=start+max_seconds
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0) for _ in range(m)] # list literal no lambda? tuple comprehension okay? It's not lambda. But no closures. Good.
    # seed as int from rng
    seed=int(rng.integers(0, 2**31-1))
    # To respect time, set maxiter based on m: 100 maybe.
    maxiter = 80 if m <= 32 else 40
    popsize = 15 if m <= 32 else 10
    res = differential_evolution(de_objective, bounds, args=(m,), maxiter=maxiter, popsize=popsize, tol=1e-6, seed=seed, init='latinhypercube')
    p=normalize(res.x)
    return p
```
But DE has no callback to stop early; maxiter limited enough. For m=64, eval per iter popsize*? maybe 10*64*? ~640, 40=>25k eval, direct conv 4096? 100M ops maybe seconds. okay.
Could run for m in [16,32,64] with 10s each; maxiter maybe lower.
Need choose time. If DE too slow, may eat budget. Use try and small maxiter. Or skip DE if no SciPy.

Interpolation:
```
def interpolate_to_length(p, n):
    m=len(p)
    if m == n: return p
    if m == 1: return np.full(n, p[0])
    # use numpy interp on centers plus endpoints
    xp=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    # p sum1, interp preserves linear but area not exactly sum? normalize.
    # To avoid zero endpoints if p[0] small? linear endpoint ok.
    xf=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    pnew=np.interp(xf, xp, p, left=p[0], right=p[-1])
    pnew=np.maximum(pnew,0.0)
    return normalize(pnew)
```
This preserves rough shape. If p coarse has a sharp spike at one point, linear interpolation spreads it, possibly lowering score (smoothing good). Good.

Maybe also use monotone interpolation? Not needed.

```
def next_pow2(m):
    m=int(m)
    if m <=1: return 1
```

`update_best`:
```
def update_best(state, p, score):
    if p is None: return
    score=float(score)
    if not np.isfinite(score): return
    if state[0] is None or score < state[1]:
        state[0] = p.copy()
        state[1] = score
    cands = state[2]
    cands.append((score, p.copy()))
    cands.sort(key=item_score_first)
    if len(cands) > 5:
        del cands[5:]
```
Potential duplicate arrays many; memory small. But sorted each update (improvements maybe thousands) sorting 5 items okay. Could only insert if score < state[1]+0.05 to avoid many duplicates; but improvements rare. If accepted but not best, not update. update called when improvement only. Good.

`local_score`:
```
def local_score(p):
    a=np.asarray(p,dtype=float)
    s=np.sum(a)
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0: return np.inf
    q=convolution(a)
    return local_score_from_q(a,q)
```
`local_score_from_q`:
```
def local_score_from_q(a,q):
    s=np.sum(a)
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0: return np.inf
    maxq=float(np.max(q))
    if not np.isfinite(maxq) or maxq <0: maxq=0.0
    return float(2.0*len(a)*maxq/(s*s))
```
Since sum1 often, could avoid sum each time; fine.

`convolution`:
```
def convolution(a):
    n=len(a)
    if n < 2:
        return np.array([float(a[0]*a[0])])
    if n <= 256:
        c=np.convolve(a,a)
    else:
        m=2*n-1
        nfft=next_pow2(m)
        fa=np.fft.rfft(a,nfft)
        fa*=fa
        c=np.fft.irfft(fa,nfft)[:m]
    c=np.asarray(c,dtype=float)
    # remove small negative
    c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)
    return c
```
`np.where` allocates; okay. Or `c[c<0]=0`.
Need if a has tiny negative? We normalize p nonnegative. Good.

`top_weights`:
```
def top_weights(q, frac):
    L=len(q)
    K=int(np.clip(frac*L, 1, L))
    if K >= L:
       return np.full(L, 1.0/L)
    w=np.zeros(L,dtype=float)
    idx=np.argpartition(q, -K)[-K:]
    w[idx]=1.0
    w /= float(K)
    return w
```
`np.argpartition` with -K where K=0? K>=1. Good. If K=L, special.
For q all zeros? argpartition arbitrary; uniform if all? Not happen.

`make_weights`:
```
def make_weights(q, frac, top_mix):
    L=len(q)
    wtop=top_weights(q, frac)
    w = top_mix*wtop
    w += (1.0-top_mix)*(1.0/L)*np.ones(L,dtype=float)
    s=w.sum()
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
       return np.full(L,1.0/L)
    return w/s
```
Density sum1. top_mix e.g. 0.95.

`gradient_for_weights`:
```
def gradient_for_weights(p,w):
    n=len(p)
    L=2*n-1
    if n <= 128:
       h=np.empty(n,dtype=float)
       for i in range(n):
           # w[i:i+n] length n? i from0 to n-1, w length 2n-1, last i=n-1 -> n-1:2n-1 exclusive length n. yes.
           h[i]=np.dot(p, w[i:i+n])
       return h
    nfft=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p,nfft)
    wr=w[::-1]
    fw=np.fft.rfft(wr,nfft)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fp*fw,nfft)
    h=c[n-1:L][::-1]
    return h
```
For n=129, L=257, nfft 512, c length512, slice [128:257] length129. Good.

`mirror_step`:
```
def mirror_step(p, grad, step, rng, sigma):
    centered = grad - np.mean(grad)
    rms = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(centered*centered)))
    if not np.isfinite(rms) or rms < 1e-18:
        return p.copy()
    direction = centered / rms
    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    logp = logp - step * direction
    if sigma and sigma > 0.0:
        logp = logp + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, len(logp))
    logp = np.clip(logp, LOG_FLOOR, 40.0)
    maxl = float(logp.max())
    pnew = np.exp(logp - maxl)
    pnew = np.maximum(pnew, 1e-15)
    s = float(pnew.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return normalize(p)
    pnew /= s
    return pnew
```
`sigma` can be 0.0; `if sigma > 0`.

`add_noise` similar.

`block_perturb`:
```
def block_perturb(p, rng, sigma):
    cand=np.asarray(p,dtype=float).copy()
    n=len(cand)
    nblocks = int(1 + rng.integers(0, 2))
    for _ in range(int(nblocks)):
        maxw=max(2, n//20)
        w=int(rng.integers(1, maxw+1))
        if n - w <= 0: w=1
        st=int(rng.integers(0, n-w+1))
        factor=float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
        factor=np.clip(factor, 1e-3, 1000.0)
        cand[st:st+w] *= factor
    return normalize(cand)
```
No lambda. `rng.integers` returns numpy int; convert.

`make_initial_candidates`: Need no use of global height? It takes n.
```
def make_initial_candidates(n):
    out=[]
    idx=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=float)
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    for a in (0.35,0.45,0.5,0.55,0.6):
       out.append(normalize(idx**(-a)))
    for r in (0.0,0.25,0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0):
       out.append(normalize(idx**(-0.5)*np.exp(-r*x)))
    for r in (0.0,0.25,0.5,1.0,1.5):
       out.append(normalize(idx**(-0.5)*(1.0+r*x)))
    for r in (0.05,0.2,0.5,1.0):
       out.append(normalize(idx**(-0.5)+r))
    left=idx**(-0.5); right=left[::-1]
    for mix in (0.2,0.4,0.5,0.6,0.8):
       out.append(normalize(mix*left+(1.0-mix)*right))
    # A bump: 1/sqrt + rough Gaussian
    center = int(0.3*n)
    width = max(3, n//10)
    bump = np.exp(-0.5*((np.arange(n)-center)/width)**2)
    out.append(normalize(idx**(-0.5) + 0.5*bump))
    out.append(normalize(idx**(-0.5) + bump))
    return out
```
Some candidates with exp(-r*x) at r=0 duplicates; scoring top okay.
If n small, `open`? okay.
Potential overflow `idx**(-0.6)` for idx1 fine.

`de_objective`:
```
def de_objective(x, m):
    p=np.asarray(x,dtype=float)
    p=np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p=np.maximum(p,0.0)
    s=float(p.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15: return 1e9
    p=p/s
    q=convolution(p)
    return float(2.0*m*float(np.max(q)))
```
Since p sum1. `m` should equal len x. If not, use len.

`de_coarse_search`:
```
def de_coarse_search(m, max_seconds, rng):
    try:
        from scipy.optimize import differential_evolution
    except Exception:
        return None
    start=time.monotonic()
    if time.monotonic() >= start + max_seconds: return None
    seed=int(rng.integers(0, 2**31-1))
    maxiter = 120 if m <= 32 else (80 if m <= 64 else 50)
    popsize = 12 if m <= 32 else (10 if m <= 64 else 8)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0) for _ in range(m)]
    try:
        res = differential_evolution(de_objective, bounds, args=(m,), maxiter=maxiter, popsize=popsize, tol=1e-6, seed=seed, init='latinhypercube')
    except Exception:
        return None
    p=normalize(res.x)
    return p
```
`init='latinhypercube'` might require popsize; okay.
Could run beyond time if maxiter high; but likely short. If concerned, use `callback` not allowed? callback can be nested closure/lambda; no. We can lower maxiter.

```
p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
# To avoid all endpoints zero if p sum positive, maybe if p[0]==0 and p[-1]==0 and interior shaped, interp endpoints 0 okay.
return normalize(pnew)
```

```
def update_best(state,p,score,tag=''):
   if p is None or not np.isfinite(score): return
   improved = state[0] is None or score < state[1]
   if improved:
       old=state[1]
       state[0]=p.copy(); state[1]=float(score)
       now=time.monotonic()
       if now - state[3] > 2.0:
           print('%s local best %.8f (from %.8f)' % (tag, score, old))
           state[3]=now
   cands=state[2]
   # insert if better than worst or len<5
   if len(cands)<5 or score < cands[-1][0]:
       cands.append((float(score), p.copy()))
       cands.sort(key=item_score_first)
       if len(cands)>5: del cands[5:]
```

```
def polish(p0, deadline, rng, state, tag='', pool=None):
    p=normalize(p0.copy())
    q=convolution(p)
    cur=local_score_from_q(p,q)
    update_best(state,p,cur,tag)
    step=0.12
    temp=1e-3
    stuck=0
    no_best=0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        frac = 0.03 + 0.45*np.exp(-it/2000.0)
        w=make_weights(q, frac, 0.95)
        grad=gradient_for_weights(p,w)
        sigma=0.0
        if it % 100 == 50:
            sigma=0.05
        p_new=mirror_step(p,grad,step,rng,sigma)
        q_new=convolution(p_new)
        sc_new=local_score_from_q(p_new,q_new)
        if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new <= cur + temp:
            p=p_new; q=q_new; cur=sc_new
            step=min(0.6, step*1.02)
            temp=max(1e-7, temp*0.999)
            stuck=0
        else:
            step=max(0.02, step*0.95)
            temp=max(1e-8, temp*0.99)
            stuck += 1
        if cur < state[1]:
            no_best=0
        else:
            no_best += 1
        if (stuck > 300 and cur > state[1] + 1e-8) or no_best > 1500:
            if pool is not None and len(pool) > 0:
                pbase=pool[int(rng.integers(0, len(pool)))]
            else:
                pbase=state[0] if state[0] is not None else p
            p=add_noise(pbase, rng, 0.2)
            q=convolution(p); cur=local_score_from_q(p,q)
            step=0.1; temp=1e-3; stuck=0; no_best=0; it=0
        it += 1
    return state
```
If `state[0]` is None? not.
`temp` units of objective: need accept worse by up to 0.001. Target difference 0.07. To escape, maybe temp 0.01. Start 0.01? If accepting too many worse, may wander. Use 0.005. Let's set `temp=0.003`. Fine.
`no_best > 1500` perhaps too many iterations; if iterations ~50k, restart rarely. Use 800. `stuck > 200` if current worsens. Good.

`block_search`:
```
def block_search(p0, deadline, rng, state, tag=''):
   p=add_noise(p0,rng,0.02)
   q=convolution(p); cur=local_score_from_q(p,q)
   update_best(state,p,cur,tag)
   sigma=0.25
   no_improve=0
   while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       c=block_perturb(p,rng,sigma)
       q=convolution(c); sc=local_score_from_q(c,q)
       if sc < cur:
          p=c; q=q; cur=sc; update_best(state,p,cur,tag); sigma=min(0.5,sigma*1.05); no_improve=0
       else:
          # occasional accept if close
          if sc <= cur + 1e-5:
              p=c; q=q; cur=sc
          else:
              no_improve +=1
              if no_improve % 50 ==0:
                 sigma=max(0.05,sigma*0.95)
   return state
```
But block perturb might slowly degrade? Accept if close. Fine.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    start=time.monotonic()
    B=float(budget_s)
    final_deadline=start + max(30.0, B - 5.0)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    # n
    n=3809
    try:
       base_seq = height_sequence_1
       arr=np.asarray(base_seq, dtype=float)
       if arr.ndim == 1 and arr.size > 0:
          n=int(arr.size)
          base_p=normalize(arr)
    except Exception:
       base_p=None
    if n < 32: n=32
    if n > 20000: n=20000
    global_state=make_best_state()
    # candidate pool fine
    inits=make_initial_candidates(n)
    if base_p is not None:
       inits.append(base_p)
    scored=[]
    for p in inits:
       if p is None: continue
       p=normalize(p)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)): continue
       sc=local_score(p)
       if np.isfinite(sc):
          scored.append((sc,p))
    scored.sort(key=item_score_first)
    pool=[]
    if scored:
       update_best(global_state, scored[0][1], scored[0][0], 'init')
       for sc,p in scored[:3]:
          pool.append(p)
    else:
       p=normalize(np.ones(n,dtype=float)/n)
       update_best(global_state,p,local_score(p),'init')
       pool.append(p)
    # prelim DE
    t_prelim=min(60.0, 0.05*B)
    de_candidates=[]
    de_deadline=start + t_prelim
    for m in (32,64,128):
       if time.monotonic() >= de_deadline: break
       remaining=max(5.0, de_deadline - time.monotonic())
       pco=de_coarse_search(m, min(20.0, remaining), rng)
       if pco is not None:
           pf=interpolate_to_length(pco,n)
           de_candidates.append(pf)
           sc=local_score(pf)
           if np.isfinite(sc):
              update_best(global_state,pf,sc,'DE')
              if len(pool)<10: pool.append(pf)
    # Coarse stage
    m_coarse=256
    t_coarse=min(300.0, 0.15*B)
    coarse_deadline=min(final_deadline - (0.8*B)?, start? ) # simpler: start + t_prelim + t_coarse
    coarse_deadline = min(final_deadline - (0.8*B + 30.0), start + t_prelim + t_coarse)
```

One more thought: The gradient for original objective `q=p*p` uses ordered pairs factor2. If we use mirror descent on log p, the update direction sign maybe should be `+`? We minimize S, gradient positive. In log variables, derivative of S wrt log p_i = p_i grad_i. Mirror descent with entropy on p often update p ← p exp(-η ∇S) not `p exp(-η p_i∇S)`. Our `logp - step*grad` uses gradient wrt p divided by p? Actually to minimize S(p) under simplex with multiplicative updates, one uses p_i ∝ p_i / ∑_k w_k p_{k-i} (for nonnegative quadratic constraints) maybe. Let's derive.
We use mirror descent in Bregman divergence D(p||q) with update p^+ = argmin S(q)+η D(q||p)? The dual gradient wrt log p is p_i ∂S/∂p_i. The natural log update is `log p_i <- log p_i - η ∂S/∂log p_i = log p_i - η p_i grad_i`. We instead use `grad_i` (not times p_i). Which is correct? We need think.
Objective S(p)=∑w_k p^T A_k p. Gradient ∂S/∂p_i = 2H_i (H_i=∑w p_{k-i}). If using gradient descent in log p (change variable y=log p, p=exp(y)/Z), the gradient of S wrt y_i = p_i ∂S/∂p_i (if sum normalization? plus). So mirror/dual update should subtract `p_i grad_i`, not grad_i. Our current update subtracts `grad_i`, which is gradient wrt p scaled by 1/p_i. That may be too aggressive for small p. For p_i tiny, grad_i could be nonzero from other p_j, subtracting grad_i changes logp a lot after normalization? Actually logp change independent of p_i, so tiny p can be increased/decreased multiplicatively by factor exp(-step*grad_i). If grad_i is O(1/n), tiny p changes by finite factor. The natural gradient p_i*grad_i would make tiny p almost no change in logp, which might be undesirable? We want adjust all coordinates multiplicatively; using `grad_i` (which equals derivative of S wrt log p if we ignore p_i?) Let's examine: If p_i is zero, derivative wrt log p_i = p_i grad_i =0, so near-zero coordinates not updated, preserving sparsity. But we may want to bring back zeros. Using grad_i allows mass to move from high to low via centered: low p_i with small grad_i gets increased if grad_i below mean, even if p_i small. This is like additive gradient in log space, not natural. It may be more exploratory. Which is better? The reference LP and multiplicative updates often use p_i *= 1/(w_k p_{k-i}) i.e., log update subtract ∑w p_{k-i} (without p_i), for minimizing constraints? For example, independent component analysis ICA uses p_i ← p_i / sqrt(constraint) maybe. For minimizing max S, subtracting H_i seems plausible: It reduces p at positions contributing to high lags, independent of current p. This can pull mass away from peaks even if p small. I think our update (not multiplied by p_i) may be good for positivity/exploration.
But the centered subtraction of a nonnatural gradient may not guarantee descent. We use accept/reject.
Could we instead use `direction = p * grad` centered? Then low p not changed; may get stuck with zeros. We prefer our `grad`.

Maybe use a blend: `direction = alpha*grad + (1-alpha)*p*grad`? But p*grad has scale O(1/n^2), much smaller; after RMS, maybe. Not.

Given empirical unknown, keep `grad`.

Another view: Our gradient H_i =∑w p_{k-i}. If we update logp_i := logp_i - η H_i, for a single active lag k, then p_i p_{k-i} pair contributions? The update reduces both coordinates in pair by factors exp(-η p_{k-i}) and exp(-η p_i), product factor exp(-η(p_i+p_{k-i})), not proportional to product. It may over-reduce if weights high. But centered keeps sum.

Could use `direction = np.log1p(grad)` for stability? no.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing

    2 * n * max(f * f) / (sum(f))^2

using FFT convolution, log-space multiplicative updates with annealed
top-lag weights, multi-resolution coarse optimization (including optional
SciPy differential evolution), interpolation to the working length, and a final
block-polish stage.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

LOG_FLOOR = -40.0
LOG_CEIL = 40.0
P_FLOOR = 1e-15


def next_pow2(m):
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def item_score_first(item):
    return item[0]


def max_abs_diff(a, b):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=float)
    return float(np.max(np.abs(a - b)))


def normalize(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=float).copy()
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        n = max(1, int(p.size))
        return np.ones(n, dtype=float) / float(n)
    p /= s
    return p


def convolution(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n < 2:
        return np.array([float(a[0] * a[0])], dtype=float)

    if n <= 256:
        c = np.convolve(a, a)
    else:
        m = 2 * n - 1
        nfft = next_pow2(m)
        fa = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
        fa *= fa
        c = np.fft.irfft(fa, nfft)[:m]

    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=float)
    c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)
    return c


def local_score_from_q(a, q):
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.inf
    maxq = float(np.max(q))
    if not np.isfinite(maxq):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * len(a) * maxq / (s * s))


def local_score(p):
    a = np.asarray(p, dtype=float)
    q = convolution(a)
    return local_score_from_q(a, q)


def top_weights(q, frac):
    L = int(q.size)
    K = int(np.clip(float(frac) * L, 1, L))
    if K >= L:
        return np.full(L, 1.0 / float(L), dtype=float)

    w = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
    idx = np.argpartition(q, -K)[-K:]
    w[idx] = 1.0
    w /= float(K)
    return w


def make_weights(q, frac, top_mix):
    L = int(q.size)
    w = top_mix * top_weights(q, frac)
    w += (1.0 - top_mix) * (1.0 / float(L)) * np.ones(L, dtype=float)
    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(L, 1.0 / float(L), dtype=float)
    return w / s


def gradient_for_weights(p, w):
    """
    Returns H_i = sum_k w_k p_{k-i} (valid indices only).
    This is one half of the gradient of sum_k w_k (p*p)_k, but the constant
    scale is removed by RMS normalization in mirror_step.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=float)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=float)
    n = int(p.size)
    L = 2 * n - 1

    if n <= 128:
        h = np.empty(n, dtype=float)
        for i in range(n):
            h[i] = np.dot(p, w[i:i + n])
        return h

    nfft = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, nfft)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], nfft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fp * fw, nfft)

    # h_i = c[(2n-2) - i], i=0..n-1
    h = c[n - 1:L][::-1]
    return h


def mirror_step(p, grad, step, rng, sigma):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=float)
    grad = np.asarray(grad, dtype=float)

    centered = grad - float(np.mean(grad))
    rms = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(centered * centered)))
    if not np.isfinite(rms) or rms < 1e-18:
        return p.copy()

    direction = centered / rms
    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    logp = logp - step * direction

    if sigma is not None and float(sigma) > 0.0:
        logp = logp + rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), len(logp))

    logp = np.clip(logp, LOG_FLOOR, LOG_CEIL)
    maxl = float(np.max(logp))
    pnew = np.exp(logp - maxl)
    pnew = np.maximum(pnew, P_FLOOR)
    s = float(np.sum(pnew))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return normalize(p)
    pnew /= s
    return pnew


def add_noise(p, rng, sigma):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=float)
    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    logp = logp + rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), len(logp))
    logp = np.clip(logp, LOG_FLOOR, LOG_CEIL)
    maxl = float(np.max(logp))
    pnew = np.exp(logp - maxl)
    pnew = np.maximum(pnew, P_FLOOR)
    return normalize(pnew)


def block_perturb(p, rng, sigma):
    cand = np.asarray(p, dtype=float).copy()
    n = int(cand.size)
    nblocks = int(1 + rng.integers(0, 2))

    for _ in range(nblocks):
        maxw = max(2, n // 20)
        w = int(rng.integers(1, maxw + 1))
        if w > n:
            w = n
        if n - w <= 0:
            w = 1
        st = int(rng.integers(0, n - w + 1))
        factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma))))
        factor = min(1000.0, max(1e-3, factor))
        cand[st:st + w] *= factor

    return normalize(cand)


def make_best_state():
    return [None, float("inf"), [], 0.0]


def update_best(state, p, score, tag=""):
    if p is None:
        return
    score = float(score)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return

    improved = (state[0] is None) or (score < state[1])
    if improved:
        old = state[1]
        state[0] = np.asarray(p, dtype=float).copy()
        state[1] = score
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now - state[3] > 2.0:
            print("%s new local best %.9f (previous %.9f)" % (tag, score, old))
            state[3] = now

    cands = state[2]
    if len(cands) < 5 or score < cands[-1][0]:
        cands.append((score, np.asarray(p, dtype=float).copy()))
        cands.sort(key=item_score_first)
        while len(cands) > 5:
            cands.pop()


def make_initial_candidates(n):
    out = []
    n = int(n)

    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=float)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)

    for a in (0.35, 0.45, 0.5, 0.55, 0.6):
        out.append(normalize(idx ** (-a)))

    for r in (0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0):
        out.append(normalize(idx ** (-0.5) * np.exp(-r * x)))

    for r in (0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5):
        out.append(normalize(idx ** (-0.5) * (1.0 + r * x)))

    for r in (0.05, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0):
        out.append(normalize(idx ** (-0.5) + r))

    left = idx ** (-0.5)
    right = left[::-1].copy()
    for mix in (0.2, 0.4, 0.5, 0.6, 0.8):
        out.append(normalize(mix * left + (1.0 - mix) * right))

    ar = np.arange(n, dtype=float)
    center = 0.30 * n
    width = max(3.0, n / 10.0)
    bump = np.exp(-0.5 * ((ar - center) / width) ** 2)
    out.append(normalize(idx ** (-0.5) + 0.5 * bump))
    out.append(normalize(idx ** (-0.5) + bump))

    center2 = 0.65 * n
    bump2 = np.exp(-0.5 * ((ar - center2) / width) ** 2)
    out.append(normalize(idx ** (-0.5) + bump2))

    return out


def de_objective(x, m):
    p = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return 1e9
    p /= s
    q = convolution(p)
    return float(2.0 * int(m) * float(np.max(q)))


def de_coarse_search(m, max_seconds, rng):
    try:
        from scipy.optimize import differential_evolution
    except Exception:
        return None

    start = time.monotonic()
    if time.monotonic() >= start + float(max_seconds):
        return None

    seed = int(rng.integers(0, 2**31 - 1))

    if m <= 32:
        maxiter = 120
        popsize = 12
    elif m <= 64:
        maxiter = 90
        popsize = 10
    else:
        maxiter = 60
        popsize = 8

    bounds = []
    for _ in range(int(m)):
        bounds.append((0.0, 1.0))

    try:
        res = differential_evolution(
            de_objective,
            bounds,
            args=(m,),
            maxiter=maxiter,
            popsize=popsize,
            tol=1e-6,
            seed=seed,
            init="latinhypercube"
        )
        return normalize(res.x)
    except Exception:
        return None


def interpolate_to_length(p, n):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=float)
    m = int(p.size)
    n = int(n)

    if m <= 1:
        return np.full(n, float(p[0]), dtype=float)
    if m == n:
        return normalize(p)

    xp = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    xf = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    pnew = np.interp(xf, xp, p, left=float(p[0]), right=float(p[-1]))
    pnew = np.maximum(pnew, 0.0)
    return normalize(pnew)


def polish(p0, deadline, rng, state, tag="", pool=None):
    p = normalize(p0)
    q = convolution(p)
    cur = local_score_from_q(p, q)
    update_best(state, p, cur, tag + "-init")

    step = 0.12
    temp = 0.005
    stuck = 0
    no_best = 0
    it = 0

    deadline = float(deadline)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        frac = 0.03 + 0.45 * np.exp(-float(it) / 2000.0)
        w = make_weights(q, frac, 0.95)
        grad = gradient_for_weights(p, w)

        sigma = 0.0
        if it % 100 == 50:
            sigma = 0.05

        p_new = mirror_step(p, grad, step, rng, sigma)
        q_new = convolution(p_new)
        sc_new = local_score_from_q(p_new, q_new)

        if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new <= cur + temp:
            p = p_new
            q = q_new
            cur = sc_new
            step = min(0.6, step * 1.02)
            temp = max(1e-7, temp * 0.999)
            stuck = 0
        else:
            step = max(0.02, step * 0.95)
            temp = max(1e-8, temp * 0.99)
            stuck += 1

        if cur < state[1]:
            no_best = 0
        else:
            no_best += 1

        if (stuck > 250 and cur > state[1] + 1e-10) or no_best > 2500:
            if pool is not None and len(pool) > 0 and float(rng.random()) < 0.30:
                base = pool[int(rng.integers(0, len(pool)))]
            else:
                base = state[0] if state[0] is not None else p
            p = add_noise(base, rng, 0.20)
            q = convolution(p)
            cur = local_score_from_q(p, q)
            step = 0.10
            temp = 0.003
            stuck = 0
            no_best = 0
            it = 0

        it += 1

    return state


def block_search(p0, deadline, rng, state, tag=""):
    p = add_noise(p0, rng, 0.02)
    q = convolution(p)
    cur = local_score_from_q(p, q)
    update_best(state, p, cur, tag + "-block-init")

    sigma = 0.25
    no_improve = 0
    deadline = float(deadline)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = block_perturb(p, rng, sigma)
        qc = convolution(c)
        sc = local_score_from_q(c, qc)

        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur:
            p = c
            q = qc
            cur = sc
            update_best(state, p, cur, tag)
            sigma = min(0.5, sigma * 1.05)
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc <= cur + 1e-6:
                p = c
                q = qc
                cur = sc
            else:
                no_improve += 1
                if no_improve % 40 == 0:
                    sigma = max(0.04, sigma * 0.94)

    return state


def official_evaluate(p):
    try:
        lst = [float(x) for x in np.asarray(p).tolist()]
        return float(evaluate_sequence(lst))
    except Exception:
        return np.inf


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    B = max(30.0, float(budget_s))
    final_deadline = start + B - 5.0

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    # Prefer the provided construction length when reasonable.
    n = 3809
    base_p = None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=float)
        if arr.ndim == 1 and arr.size > 0:
            n = int(arr.size)
            base_p = normalize(arr)
    except Exception:
        pass

    if n < 32:
        n = 32
    if n > 8192:
        n = 8192

    global_state = make_best_state()

    # Fine-length initial candidates.
    inits = make_initial_candidates(n)
    if base_p is not None:
        inits.append(base_p)

    scored = []
    for p in inits:
        if p is None:
            continue
        p = normalize(p)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
            continue
        sc = local_score(p)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((sc, p))

    scored.sort(key=item_score_first)

    pool = []
    if scored:
        update_best(global_state, scored[0][1], scored[0][0], "fine-init")
        for _, p in scored[:3]:
            pool.append(p)
    else:
        p = normalize(np.ones(n, dtype=float))
        update_best(global_state, p, local_score(p), "fallback")
        pool.append(p)

    # Time allocation.
    t_prelim = 0.05 * B
    t_coarse = 0.15 * B
    t_fine1 = 0.50 * B
    t_fine2 = 0.18 * B
    # remaining is final block + safety margin

    d_prelim = start + t_prelim
    d_coarse = d_prelim + t_coarse
    d_fine1 = d_coarse + t_fine1
    d_fine2 = d_fine1 + t_fine2
    d_final = min(final_deadline, start + B - 10.0)

    # Preliminary coarse DE candidates, if useful.
    de_candidates = []
    de_end = min(d_prelim, d_coarse - 60.0)
    for m in (32, 64, 128):
        if time.monotonic() >= de_end:
            break
        remaining = max(2.0, de_end - time.monotonic())
        pco = de_coarse_search(m, min(20.0, remaining), rng)
        if pco is not None:
            pf = interpolate_to_length(pco, n)
            sc = local_score(pf)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                de_candidates.append(pf)
                update_best(global_state, pf, sc, "DE")
                if len(pool) < 10:
                    pool.append(pf)

    # Coarse optimization.
    m_coarse = 256
    coarse_state = make_best_state()
    coarse_inits = make_initial_candidates(m_coarse)

    if base_p is not None:
        coarse_inits.append(interpolate_to_length(base_p, m_coarse))
    for p in de_candidates:
        coarse_inits.append(interpolate_to_length(p, m_coarse))

    coarse_scored = []
    for p in coarse_inits:
        p = normalize(p)
        sc = local_score(p)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            coarse_scored.append((sc, p))

    coarse_scored.sort(key=item_score_first)
    coarse_pool = []
    if coarse_scored:
        update_best(coarse_state, coarse_scored[0][1], coarse_scored[0][0], "coarse-init")
        for _, p in coarse_scored[:3]:
            coarse_pool.append(p)

    if d_coarse > time.monotonic() + 30.0:
        coarse_start = coarse_state[0] if coarse_state[0] is not None else coarse_scored[0][1]
        polish(coarse_start, min(d_coarse, d_final - (0.55 * B)), rng, coarse_state, "coarse", coarse_pool)

    # Bring coarse candidates back to fine length.
    for _, p in coarse_state[2]:
        pf = interpolate_to_length(p, n)
        sc = local_score(pf)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            update_best(global_state, pf, sc, "coarse->fine")
            if len(pool) < 12:
                pool.append(pf)

    # Build up to two distinct fine branches.
    branch_scores = []
    if global_state[0] is not None:
        branch_scores.append((global_state[1], global_state[0]))
    for _, p in scored[:3]:
        branch_scores.append((local_score(p), p))
    for p in pool:
        branch_scores.append((local_score(p), p))
    branch_scores.sort(key=item_score_first)

    branches = []
    for sc, p in branch_scores:
        if len(branches) >= 2:
            break
        distinct = True
        for q in branches:
            if p.shape == q.shape and max_abs_diff(p, q) < 1e-4:
                distinct = False
                break
        if distinct:
            branches.append(p)

    if len(branches) == 0:
        if global_state[0] is not None:
            branches.append(global_state[0])
        else:
            branches.append(normalize(np.ones(n, dtype=float)))

    # Main fine search from current best.
    if d_fine1 > time.monotonic() + 30.0:
        polish(branches[0], min(d_fine1, d_final - (0.25 * B)), rng, global_state, "fine1", pool)

    # Second fine branch from a distinct candidate, if time remains.
    if len(branches) > 1 and d_fine2 > time.monotonic() + 30.0:
        polish(branches[1], min(d_fine2, d_final - 30.0), rng, global_state, "fine2", pool)

    # Final local block polish.
    if d_final > time.monotonic() + 10.0:
        pstart = global_state[0] if global_state[0] is not None else branches[0]
        block_search(pstart, d_final, rng, global_state, "final")

    # Final official verification of a few candidates.
    candidates = []
    if global_state[0] is not None:
        candidates.append(global_state[0])
    for _, p in global_state[2]:
        candidates.append(p)
    if scored:
        candidates.append(scored[0][1])
    candidates.append(normalize(np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=float) ** (-0.5)))

    # For larger sequences, official evaluation is expensive; test few.
    max_official = 3 if n > 6000 else 8
    best_p = None
    best_off = np.inf

    for p in candidates:
        if best_off == 0.0:
            break
        if p is None:
            continue
        if len(candidates) > max_official and len(best_p for _ in candidates) is None:
            pass
        p = normalize(p)
        off = official_evaluate(p)
        if np.isfinite(off) and off < best_off:
            best_off = off
            best_p = p

    if best_p is None:
        best_p = normalize(np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=float) ** (-0.5))

    out = np.asarray(best_p, dtype=float)
    out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
